Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files.     "structuredContent": {
```json
      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"experiments\": {\"plan016-extended-1000-s42\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-14T05:29:47.634Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-15T20:25:41.946Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Extended training 1000 steps exec-only: plan001-s42 500步 PR=93% 是真收敛还是 lucky early convergence? 1000步观察 PR 和 ISR 在更长 horizon 的行为。s43(14%)/s44(98% tautological) 在 500 步均失败，延长训练是否能改善？\", \"parent\": \"plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\", \"metrics\": {\"eval_proved_only_isr\": 0.0028, \"eval_mean_isr\": 0.0027, \"eval_isr_std\": 0.0118, \"eval_median_isr\": 0, \"eval_max_isr\": 0.0588, \"eval_prove_rate\": 0.96, \"eval_n_proved\": 96, \"eval_n_total\": 100}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"Extended training to 1000 steps CONFIRMS ISR collapse worsens monotonically.\\\\n\\\\n## 500 vs 1000 step comparison (eval_sta.py):\\\\n| Metric | plan001-s42 (500步) | plan016 (1000步) | Change |\\\\n|--------|-------------------|-----------------|--------|\\\\n| eval PR | 0.93 | 0.96 | +3.2% |\\\\n| eval ISR | 0.123 | 0.0027 | -97.8% |\\\\n| proved_only ISR | 0.132 | 0.0028 | -97.9% |\\\\n| components | 6.58 | 18.55 | +182% |\\\\n| ISR=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n| n_necessary=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n\\\\n## Key findings:\\\\n1. PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Model generates longer proofs with more components, but 94.8% have ZERO necessary components.\\\\n3. NOT tautological collapse (comp=2 ratio=0). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. Windowed ISR trend is monotonically decreasing: 0.1115 (101-200) → 0.0433 (401-500) → 0.0200 (601-700) → 0.0144 (901-1000). No recovery signal.\\\\n5. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. The 500-step ISR of 0.123 was not a floor — it was a snapshot of ongoing collapse.\"}, \"plan018-additive-random-b01-s44-r2\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-15T14:17:54.315Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-15T19:34:24.620Z\", \"metrics\": {\"train_prove_rate\": 0.64675, \"train_mean_isr\": 0.08092, \"train_loss\": 0.00937, \"train_steps\": 500, \"train_runtime_sec\": 4998, \"eval_prove_rate\": 0.89, \"eval_mean_isr\": 0.0619, \"eval_isr_std\": 0.1756, \"eval_median_isr\": 0, \"eval_isr_pr_ratio\": 0.0695, \"eval_proved_only_isr\": 0.0695, \"eval_n_proved\": 89, \"eval_n_problems\": 100, \"h7_count\": 0, \"h7a_count\": 0, \"h7b_count\": 0, \"h1_count\": 73, \"h8_count\": 16, \"genuine_count\": 6, \"genuine_rate\": 0.067, \"h7_rate\": 0, \"h1_rate\": 0.82, \"h8_rate\": 0.18, \"content_independent_count\": 18}, \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"plan018-additive-random-b01-s43-r3\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-15T14:17:51.220Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-15T18:57:50.838Z\", \"metrics\": {\"prove_rate\": 0.94, \"n_proved\": 94, \"n_problems\": 100, \"mean_isr\": 0.1406, \"isr_std\": null, \"proved_only_isr\": 0.1495, \"h7_count\": 36, \"h7_rate\": 0.383, \"h7a_count\": 36, \"h7b_count\": 0, \"h1_count\": 56, \"h1_rate\": 0.596, \"h8_count\": 1, \"h8_rate\": 0.011, \"genuine_count\": 1, \"genuine_rate\": 0.0106, \"train_prove_rate\": 0.5825, \"train_mean_isr\": 0.1266, \"taxonomy_tool\": \"classify_h7.py + manual subtype analysis (2026-05-15)\", \"taxonomy_note\": \"D093 H7 subtype: all 36 H7 are H7a (text-match tautology), 0 H7b. single_premise_count=0: Random H7a are multi-premise proofs with embedded tautological premise, structurally different from exec-only H7a (single-premise minimal tautology). ```text
```
```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation: ISR reveals a 99.5\% collapse under exec-only GRPO that PR hides, while SFT localizes the failure to RL optimization (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
5	Repurposed as a training signal, ISR induces meta-level Goodhart's Law: at $\beta{=}0.1$, it amplifies H7 tautological gaming ${\sim}5\times$ ($70.6\%$ vs.\ $13.7\%$ random, $p{<}10^{-39}$), and the sole apparent ISR gain vanishes ($1.06\times$) after controlling for premise count; at $\beta{=}0.3$, each seed converges to a distinct failure mode ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$).
6	The pattern transfers to Prolog (3/3 seeds, 0\% non-hacking) via distinct mechanisms including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
7	ISR semantics are objective-dependent: exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals redundancy; ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals metric gaming.
8	
9	\paragraph{Classifier blind spots.}
10	H7b (obfuscated tautology) and Prolog query-as-fact gaming demonstrate that text-match classifiers have systematic blind spots exploitable by optimization.
11	In both cases, functional LOO analysis exposed gaming invisible to surface-level detection---H7b via vacuous quantifier wrapping ($10/12$ at $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.83$, triple gaming of execution, ISR, and classifier), query-as-fact via $95/97$ tautological samples the strict classifier labeled non-hacking (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
12	Both cases argue for mandatory functional cross-validation of text-based hacking classifiers.
13	
14	\paragraph{Hacking mode switching.}
15	Under identical reward configurations, different seeds converge to qualitatively different modes (Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}): random $\beta{=}0.1$ ranges from H8-enriched (s44) to H7-dominant (s43) to highest non-hacking (s42); ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ similarly switches between H1-heavy (s42) and near-pure H7 (s43/s44, ${\geq}97\%$).
16	Single-seed results can therefore misrepresent a reward configuration's hacking profile; multi-seed evaluation with per-mode reporting should be standard.
17	
18	The asymmetry---reliable observer, unreliable optimizer---may generalize to other perturbation-based diagnostics; ISR and similar metrics should serve as post-hoc evaluation tools, with future work targeting gaming-robust rewards and broader logic types.
19	
```

</details>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Neurosymbolic formalizers trained with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) achieve high prove rates---93\% on FOLIO---yet 87\% of proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises, a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution reward.
3	We introduce \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic that applies leave-one-out component necessity testing to solver inputs.
4	The fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR): ISR$=$1 indicates full necessity; ISR$=$0 complete redundancy.
5	ISR is uncorrelated with downstream NLI accuracy ($\rho{=}{-}0.049$), confirming it measures structural---not semantic---integrity.
6	As a diagnostic, ISR exposes a collapse trajectory hidden from PR: over 100--1000 GRPO steps (Qwen3-4B), ISR falls $0.642{\to}0.003$ while PR rises $32\%{\to}96\%$; a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) baseline reaches 92.3\% non-hacking on identical data (3-seed pooled), localizing the failure to RL.
7	As a reward, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta\,\text{ISR})$ at $\beta{=}0.1$ induces \emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 70.6\% conclusion duplication (H7) versus 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$; random H7 is seed-dependent, driven by one of three seeds); ISR amplifies H7 frequency ${\sim}5{\times}$ rather than exclusively causing it; the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound; $\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training regardless of signal source (ISR: PR $66{\pm}42\%$; random: $54{\pm}35\%$), with individual seeds collapsing to as low as $18\%$.
8	Cross-logic transfer to Prolog yields universal Goodhart: 3/3 seeds gaming via distinct mechanisms, 0\% non-hacking.
9	Across 6 reward conditions, 2 logic types, 3 seeds, we catalog 8 hacking modes---including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers---documenting ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as a diagnostic, unreliable as an optimization target.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
10	\end{abstract}
11	
```

_source line 26 · 2026-05-16T01:39:38.148Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NHyzc4CJjr3zYf5yEdph5y`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	A GRPO-trained formalizer achieves a 93\% prove rate on first-order logic problems---yet 87\% of its proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises.
5	The solver succeeds not because the model has learned to reason, but because the formalization trivially entails any conclusion already appearing as a premise.
6	This structural shortcut is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation or degenerate circular embedding.
7	
8	\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}
9	Prove rate---the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}---conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
10	Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.
11	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
12	A separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
13	
14	\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}
15	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying \emph{component necessity testing}---leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs---to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}).
16	For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), with $\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.
17	LOO ablation itself is standard~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; novelty lies in its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and in the empirical phenomena it surfaces.
18	ISR captures structural rather than semantic integrity (downstream NLI: $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).
19	
20	\paragraph{ISR as diagnostic.}
21	As a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR: $\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\,0.642\to 0.132\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (99.5\% degradation) while PR rises $32\%{\to}96\%$.
22	Three seeds diverge radically (PR $\sigma{=}47$\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse.
23	An SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\text{PR}{=}13.0\%$ with 92.3\% non-hacking (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), localizing the failure to RL optimization, not data or capacity.
24	
25	\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart.}
26	Repurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, ISR is gamed at its own definition---\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.
27	At $\beta{=}0.1$, 70.6\% of proved samples exhibit conclusion duplication (H7)---versus 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$; random H7 is highly seed-dependent), showing ISR amplifies tautological collapse by ${\sim}5{\times}$; the one seed with a non-trivial residual reduces to $1.06\times$ random after controlling for a premise-count confound.
28	At $\beta{=}0.3$, both ISR and random produce extreme cross-seed instability (PR $66{\pm}42\%$ and $54{\pm}35\%$, respectively), confirming that high-dose proxy weighting is harmful regardless of signal content; ISR seeds diverge into three distinct pathways (reward collapse, shortcut dominance, pure metric gaming) while random converges uniformly to H1.
29	The effect transfers and intensifies in Prolog: 3/3 seeds yield 0\% non-hacking via distinct mechanisms, including \emph{query-as-fact gaming} that bypasses text-match classifiers.
30	ISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \emph{reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.}
31	
32	\paragraph{Contributions.}
33	Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\textbf{C1}) ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, with a 99.5\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR and an SFT control localizing the failure to RL (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\textbf{C2}) empirical meta-level Goodhart, with a random control showing ISR amplifies H7 gaming ${\sim}5{\times}$ (70.6\% vs.\ 13.7\%, Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$; random H7 is seed-dependent) and a premise-count-matched analysis showing the sole apparent gain is an LOO artifact (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); (\textbf{C3}) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with universal Goodhart (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\textbf{C4}) an eight-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming and obfuscated tautology, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
34	These are cautionary results for any work treating a structural proxy as a training signal.
35	
```

```json
      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/introduction.tex",
      "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nA GRPO-trained formalizer achieves a 93\\% prove rate on first-order logic problems---yet 87\\% of its proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises.\nThe solver succeeds not because the model has learned to reason, but because the formalization trivially entails any conclusion already appearing as a premise.\nThis structural shortcut is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation or degenerate circular embedding.\n\n\\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}\nProve rate---the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}---conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.\nRecent work applies GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.\nProcess reward models~\\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.\nA separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \\emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.\n\n\\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}\nWe address this gap with \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying \\emph{component necessity testing}---leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs---to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\\ref{fig:sta-overview}).\nFor each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), with $\\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.\nLOO ablation itself is standard~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; novelty lies in its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and in the empirical phenomena it surfaces.\nISR captures structural rather than semantic integrity (downstream NLI: $\\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).\n\n\\paragraph{ISR as diagnostic.}\nAs a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR: $\\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\\,0.642\\to 0.132\\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (99.5\\% degradation) while PR rises $32\\%{\\to}96\\%$.\nThree seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}47$\\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse.\nAn SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ with 92.3\\% non-hacking (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), localizing the failure to RL optimization, not data or capacity.\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart.}\nRepurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\\text{exec}} \\cdot (1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, ISR is gamed at its own definition---\\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 70.6\\% of proved samples exhibit conclusion duplication (H7)---versus 13.7\\% under matched random (Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$; random H7 is highly seed-dependent), showing ISR amplifies tautological collapse by ${\\sim}5{\\times}$; the one seed with a non-trivial residual reduces to $1.06\\times$ random after controlling for a premise-count confound.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, both ISR and random produce extreme cross-seed instability (PR $66{\\pm}42\\%$ and $54{\\pm}35\\%$, respectively), confirming that high-dose proxy weighting is harmful regardless of signal content; ISR seeds diverge into three distinct pathways (reward collapse, shortcut dominance, pure metric gaming) while random converges uniformly to H1.\nThe effect transfers and intensifies in Prolog: 3/3 seeds yield 0\\% non-hacking via distinct mechanisms, including \\emph{query-as-fact gaming} that bypasses text-match classifiers.\nISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \\emph{reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.}\n\n\\paragraph{Contributions.}\nAcross 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\\textbf{C1}) ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, with a 99.5\\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR and an SFT control localizing the failure to RL (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\\textbf{C2}) empirical meta-level Goodhart, with a random control showing ISR amplifies H7 gaming ${\\sim}5{\\times}$ (70.6\\% vs.\\ 13.7\\%, Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$; random H7 is seed-dependent) and a premise-count-matched analysis showing the sole apparent gain is an LOO artifact (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); (\\textbf{C3}) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with universal Goodhart (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\\textbf{C4}) an eight-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming and obfuscated tautology, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\nThese are cautionary results for any work treating a structural proxy as a training signal.\n",
      "numLines": 35,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 35
    }
  },
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=0.92\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sta_overview.pdf}
7	\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA). (a) Formalization pipeline: NL is formalized and verified by an external solver. (b) STA protocol: leave-one-out ablation on proved formalizations yields ISR. (c) ISR as diagnostic vs.\ as reward: when the diagnostic becomes the target, meta-level Goodhart's Law emerges.}
8	\label{fig:sta-overview}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}
12	\label{sec:task}
13	
14	Given premises $P_1,\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$ verified by an external solver $S\in\{\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}a).
15	We train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
16	This optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \emph{structural quality}: a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.
17	All experiments draw from FOLIO~\citep{folio}; the held-out evaluation file retains a legacy \texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_*.jsonl} filename (from ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter}) but contains only FOLIO-derived examples.
18	
19	\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
20	\label{sec:sta}
21	
22	STA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually necessary for proof success (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}b).
23	Given a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\setminus\{c_i\}$ and re-runs the solver (Algorithm~\ref{alg:sta}).
24	A component is \emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure.
25	What constitutes a component depends on the logic: premises in FOL, clauses or ground facts in Prolog.
26	LOO ablation itself is standard in mutation testing and sensitivity analysis~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; novelty lies in its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and the empirical phenomena it surfaces.
27	
28	\begin{algorithm}[t]
29	\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
30	\label{alg:sta}
31	\begin{algorithmic}[1]
32	\REQUIRE Proved formalization $x = \{c_1, \ldots, c_n\}$, solver $S$
33	\ENSURE $\text{ISR}(x) \in [0, 1]$
34	\STATE $\textit{necessary} \gets 0$
35	\FOR{$i = 1$ to $n$}
36	    \IF{$S(x \setminus \{c_i\}) = \textit{failed}$}
37	        \STATE $\textit{necessary} \gets \textit{necessary} + 1$
38	    \ENDIF
39	\ENDFOR
40	\RETURN $\textit{necessary} \,/\, n$
41	\end{algorithmic}
42	\end{algorithm}
43	
44	\paragraph{Intensional Structural Reward.}
45	ISR is the fraction of necessary components:
46	\begin{equation}
47	  \text{ISR}(x) = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\bigl[S(x\setminus\{c_i\})=\textit{failed}\bigr]
48	  \label{eq:isr}
49	\end{equation}
50	ISR is defined only for proved formalizations: ISR${=}1$ means every component is necessary; ISR${=}0$ means complete redundancy.
51	We report \emph{proved-only} ISR (denoted ISR$^*$) throughout to avoid conflating structural quality with prove rate.
52	
53	\paragraph{Limitations of ISR.}
54	ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a formalization can achieve $\text{ISR}{=}1$ while remaining semantically unrelated to the NL input (e.g., a single premise identical to the conclusion).
55	ISR is also sensitive to component count $n$: when $n{=}2$, $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ regardless of proof quality.
56	We control for this confound via premise-count-matched analysis in \S\ref{sec:goodhart}.
57	The $O(n)$ per-clause LOO ablation is necessary: a cheaper $O(1)$ binary variant produces near-zero ISR scores ($\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$) indistinguishable from exec-only, because models generate redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.
58	However, the $O(1)$ variant includes a tautology check (conclusion provable under empty premises $\to$ ISR${=}0$) absent from exec-only, introducing a training-time confound that depresses prove rate ($63\%$ vs.\ $93\%$ exec-only); the two conditions are therefore non-equivalent in training dynamics despite producing comparable ISR signals (Appendix~\ref{app:o1-ablation}).
59	
60	\subsection{Reward Conditions and Hacking Modes}
61	\label{sec:rewards}
62	\label{sec:taxonomy-method}
63	
64	We study six conditions: exec-only ($r_{\text{exec}}$), ISR low/high-dose ($r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta\,\text{ISR})$ at $\beta\in\{0.1,0.3\}$), matched random controls ($r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta u)$, $u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$), and a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) baseline (negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations).
65	Multiplicative bonuses apply only to proved samples, keeping the prove-rate floor intact.
66	Random isolates ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects; SFT isolates whether structural degeneration arises from RL optimization or data exposure alone.
67	Each RL condition uses seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ and identical training data.
68	An earlier convex formulation ($\alpha r_{\text{exec}} + \beta r_{\text{exec}}\text{ISR}$, $\alpha{+}\beta{=}1$) was discarded because $\text{ISR}{<}1$ always penalized relative to exec-only, collapsing PR (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}).
69	
70	\paragraph{Hacking taxonomy.}
71	Across conditions, seeds, and logic types we identify eight structural-degeneration modes (full definitions in Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}):
72	\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedded among multiple premises),
73	\textbf{H7} (single-premise conclusion duplication, subtypes H7a standard and H7b obfuscated tautology; targets the ISR signal directly, since removing the sole premise forces $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$),
74	\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming: each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}),
75	\textbf{tautological collapse} (minimal two-component $P{\to}P$ without explicit ISR inflation),
76	\textbf{repetition collapse} (identical clauses repeated to inflate component count),
77	\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (elaborate but vacuous premises),
78	\textbf{contradiction explosion} (contradictory premises trivializing any conclusion via \emph{ex falso}), and
79	\textbf{query-as-fact gaming} (Prolog-specific: emit the NL question as an atom asserted as both fact and query; evades text-match classifiers).
80	Modes are identified by a two-stage procedure: strict normalized text matching (H1/H7) plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution (catches modes evading text matching).
81	We report both classifications in \S\ref{sec:experiments}.
82	
83	In interpreting ISR results, we distinguish two failure semantics that share a numerical signature: an exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals \emph{redundancy} (e.g., repeated ground facts in Prolog), whereas an ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals \emph{metric gaming} (H7-style constructions).
84	A bare ISR difference therefore does not translate directly into a reasoning-ability difference; the mode breakdown is what licenses such interpretation.
85	
```

(c) ISR as diagnostic vs.\\ as reward: when the diagnostic becomes the target, meta-level Goodhart's Law emerges.}\n\\label{fig:sta-overview}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}\n\\label{sec:task}\n\nGiven premises $P_1,\\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\\{c_1,\\ldots,c_n\\}$ verified by an external solver $S\\in\\{\\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\\}$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:sta-overview}a).\nWe train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\\text{exec}}(x) = \\mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\\textit{proved}]$.\nThis optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \\emph{structural quality}: a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.\nAll experiments draw from FOLIO~\\citep{folio}; the held-out evaluation file retains a legacy \\texttt{proofwriter\\_fol\\_*.jsonl} filename (from ProofWriter~\\citep{proofwriter}) but contains only FOLIO-derived examples.\n\n\\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{sec:sta}\n\nSTA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually necessary for proof success (Figure~\\ref{fig:sta-overview}b).\nGiven a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\\setminus\\{c_i\\}$ and re-runs the solver (Algorithm~\\ref{alg:sta}).\nA component is \\emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure.\nWhat constitutes a component depends on the logic: premises in FOL, clauses or ground facts in Prolog.\nLOO ablation itself is standard in mutation testing and sensitivity analysis~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; novelty lies in its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and the empirical phenomena it surfaces.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[t]\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{alg:sta}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\REQUIRE Proved formalization $x = \\{c_1, \\ldots, c_n\\}$, solver $S$\n\\ENSURE $\\text{ISR}(x) \\in [0, 1]$\n\\STATE $\\textit{necessary} \\gets 0$\n\\FOR{$i = 1$ to $n$}\n    \\IF{$S(x \\setminus \\{c_i\\}) = \\textit{failed}$}\n        \\STATE $\\textit{necessary} \\gets \\textit{necessary} + 1$\n    \\ENDIF\n\\ENDFOR\n\\RETURN $\\textit{necessary} \\,/\\, n$\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\\paragraph{Intensional Structural Reward.}\nISR is the fraction of necessary components:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\text{ISR}(x) = \\frac{1}{n}\\sum_{i=1}^{n}\\mathbf{1}\\bigl[S(x\\setminus\\{c_i\\})=\\textit{failed}\\bigr]\n  \\label{eq:isr}\n\\end{equation}\nISR is defined only for proved formalizations: ISR${=}1$ means every component is necessary; ISR${=}0$ means complete redundancy.\nWe report \\emph{proved-only} ISR (denoted ISR$^*$) throughout to avoid conflating structural quality with prove rate.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations of ISR.}\nISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a formalization can achieve $\\text{ISR}{=}1$ while remaining semantically unrelated to the NL input (e.g., a single premise identical to the conclusion).\nISR is also sensitive to component count $n$: when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ regardless of proof quality.\nWe control for this confound via premise-count-matched analysis in \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}.\nThe $O(n)$ per-clause LOO ablation is necessary: a cheaper $O(1)$ binary variant produces near-zero ISR scores ($\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$) indistinguishable from exec-only, because models generate redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.\nHowever, the $O(1)$ variant includes a tautology check (conclusion provable under empty premises $\\to$ ISR${=}0$) absent from exec-only, introducing a training-time confound that depresses prove rate ($63\\%$ vs.\\ $93\\%$ exec-only); the two conditions are therefore non-equivalent in training dynamics despite producing comparable ISR signals (Appendix~\\ref{app:o1-ablation}).\n\n\\subsection{Reward Conditions and Hacking Modes}\n\\label{sec:rewards}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy-method}\n\nWe study six conditions: exec-only ($r_{\\text{exec}}$), ISR low/high-dose ($r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1{+}\\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$ at $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$), matched random controls ($r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1{+}\\beta u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$), and a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) baseline (negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations).\nMultiplicative bonuses apply only to proved samples, keeping the prove-rate floor intact.\nRandom isolates ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects; SFT isolates whether structural degeneration arises from RL optimization or data exposure alone.\nEach RL condition uses seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ and identical training data.\nAn earlier convex formulation ($\\alpha r_{\\text{exec}} + \\beta r_{\\text{exec}}\\text{ISR}$, $\\alpha{+}\\beta{=}1$) was discarded because $\\text{ISR}{<}1$ always penalized relative to exec-only, collapsing PR (Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}).\n\n\\paragraph{Hacking taxonomy.}\nAcross conditions, seeds, and logic types we identify eight structural-degeneration modes (full definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}):\n\\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedded among multiple premises),\n\\textbf{H7} (single-premise conclusion duplication, subtypes H7a standard and H7b obfuscated tautology; targets the ISR signal directly, since removing the sole premise forces $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$),\n\\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming: each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}),\n\\textbf{tautological collapse} (minimal two-component $P{\\to}P$ without explicit ISR inflation),\n\\textbf{repetition collapse} (identical clauses repeated to inflate component count),\n\\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (elaborate but vacuous premises),\n\\textbf{contradiction explosion} (contradictory premises trivializing any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso}), and\n\\textbf{query-as-fact gaming} (Prolog-specific: emit the NL question as an atom asserted as both fact and query; evades text-match classifiers).\nModes are identified by a two-stage procedure: strict normalized text matching (H1/H7) plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution (catches modes evading text matching).\nWe report both classifications in \\S\\ref{sec:experiments}.\n\nIn interpreting ISR results, we distinguish two failure semantics that share a numerical signature: an exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy} (e.g., repeated ground facts in Prolog), whereas an ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (H7-style constructions).\nA bare ISR difference therefore does not translate directly into a reasoning-ability difference; the mode breakdown is what licenses such interpretation.\n",

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward, where it exhibits meta-level Goodhart's Law (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
5	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\footnote{All experiments use \texttt{data/train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}
10	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_100.jsonl}---legacy filename, no ProofWriter data) with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator (\texttt{eval\_sta.py}) so training-log ISR is never compared across runs.
11	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate---the residual of proved samples matching none of the eight cataloged patterns, a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness.
12	
13	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
14	\label{sec:diagnostic}
15

By step 1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have $\text{ISR}{=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}

23	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
24	Figure~\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) at 100, 500, 1000 steps.
25	PR rises $32\%\to93\%\to96\%$, a trajectory that execution-only evaluation would interpret as successful optimization; $\text{ISR}^*$ tells the opposite story, $0.642\to0.132\to0.003$ (214$\times$ collapse). %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
26	By 500 steps, 87.1\% of proved samples embed the conclusion (H1); by 1000 steps, the embedding rate saturates at 100\% and mean component count more than doubles ($6.58\to18.55$) as the model emits contradictory or vacuous premises that trivially derive any conclusion.
27	Extended training, lacking a structural signal, \emph{worsens} formalization quality while improving the headline number.

30	The three exec-only seeds diverge into qualitatively different failure modes despite shared data, hyperparameters, and reward (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}): s42 reaches $\text{PR}{=}93\%$ via conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}{=}87\%$); s43 collapses to $\text{PR}{=}13\%$ via repetition (87\% of rollouts truncated); s44 reaches $\text{PR}{=}98\%$ via tautological collapse ($\text{H7}{=}100\%$, mean $n{=}2.04$). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44

36	The near-absence of hacking under identical data exposure is consistent with hacking being RL-specific.

39	\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}
40	\label{sec:goodhart}
41	
42	We now reverse roles: instead of treating ISR as an observer, we use it as a reward augmentation, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, with a matched random control, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,u)$, $u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, to isolate ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects.
43	The headline finding: ISR-as-reward produces \emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming.

69	H7 tautological collapse occurs under both ISR and random conditions, but ISR amplifies it ${\sim}5\times$: $70.6\%$ ($163/231$) under ISR vs.\ $13.7\%$ ($36/262$) under random (Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$, $\text{OR}{=}15.05$). %% registry: plan002 vs plan018 all seeds; D095 FINAL
70	Random H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\%$ in seed~43 vs.\ $0\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\%$, Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).
71	H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by construction (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.
72	Total conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}+\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\%$ across both conditions (pooled: ISR $89\%$, random $85\%$).
73	The composition differs: H1 alone accounts for $71\%$ under random vs.\ $18\%$ under ISR---ISR converts H1 into H7 rather than reducing total embedding (Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).

104	\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}
105	\label{sec:crosslogic}
106	
107	\begin{figure}[t]
108	\centering
109	\includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}
110	\caption{Cross-logic results. \textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\ Prolog exec-only $\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}
111	\label{fig:prolog-comparison}
112	\end{figure}
113	
114	We replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?
115	Detailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.
116	
117	\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}
118	Two of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\text{ISR}^{*}{\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3
119	Inspection reveals \emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.
120	This is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.
121	We are careful to distinguish: exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals \emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals \emph{metric gaming} (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.
122	
123	\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}
124	The Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).
125	Seed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42
126	Seed~43: \emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \texttt{MARY\_IS\_AN\_AVA\-DEOTE.}); strict text-match returns $\text{H7}{=}0$ but functional analysis shows all 38 proved samples have $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$ (functionally H7). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s43
127	Seed~44: \emph{query-as-fact gaming}---the model transforms the NL question into a snake-case atom asserted as both fact and query (e.g., \texttt{is\_the\_dog\_furry.}), with $95/97$ proved samples at $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$. %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s44
128	Seed~44 evaded our initial strict classifier (reported $100\%$ non-hacking); functional cross-validation exposed it---a methodological lesson that text-match classifiers have blind spots and STA's necessity profile is the cross-check.
129	We attribute Prolog's stronger collapse to its simpler operational semantics: a broader gaming basin makes the non-hacking residual disappear---corroborating evidence for Claim 2.

160	\end{tabular}}
161	\caption{Eight hacking modes across conditions (\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random: pooled across 3 seeds). H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}14$; primarily s44). Cells with seed identifiers indicate the mode dominates that seed. $^\sharp$Prolog row pools strict+functional (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).}
162	\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}
163	\end{table}
164	
165	The eight modes (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; definitions in Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span 2 logic types and 6 reward conditions.
166	The three dominant modes---\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding, GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\%$), \textbf{H7} (conclusion duplication, ISR-amplified ${\sim}5\times$; Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$), and \textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming; Appendix~\ref{app:h8})---account for $>90\%$ of hacking.
167	H7 includes an obfuscated subtype \textbf{H7b} ($14/231$) that evades text-match classifiers via vacuous quantifier wrapping, simultaneously gaming execution reward, ISR, and the hacking detector (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
168	The cross-condition pattern: \emph{reward formulation determines hacking composition but not prevalence}---all GRPO conditions show $\geq 80\%$ pooled hacking; only SFT achieves $92.3\%$ non-hacking.
169	No GRPO condition produces a high-PR, majority non-hacking run; the taxonomy is descriptive, not exhaustive (query-as-fact gaming was discovered only via functional cross-validation).
170
```

```json
      "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward, where it exhibits meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nA hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B~\\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\\footnote{All experiments use \\texttt{data/train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}\nHeld-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\\texttt{proofwriter\\_fol\\_100.jsonl}---legacy filename, no ProofWriter data) with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator (\\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}) so training-log ISR is never compared across runs.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \\emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate---the residual of proved samples matching none of the eight cataloged patterns, a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$93\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls $0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$ over 100$\\to$500$\\to$1000 steps. $32\\%\\to93\\%\\to96\\%$, a trajectory that execution-only evaluation would interpret as successful optimization; $\\text{ISR}^*$ tells the opposite story, $0.642\\to0.132\\to0.003$ (214$\\times$ D095 FINAL\nRandom H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\\%$ in seed~43 vs.\\ $0\\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\\%$, Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).\nH7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by construction (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both conditions (pooled: ISR $89\\%$, random $85\\%$).\nThe composition differs: H1 alone accounts for $71\\%$ under random vs.\\ $18\\%$ under ISR---ISR converts H1 into H7 rather than reducing total embedding (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nRandom also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\\pm}8\\%$ vs.\\ $77{\\pm}14\\%$).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.\nThis appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by definition.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nAfter reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), following the pattern described by~\\citet{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}4\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); diff --git a/paper/abstract.tex b/paper/abstract.tex
```

```text
+++ b/paper/abstract.tex
@@ -1,10 +1,10 @@
 \begin{abstract}
-GRPO-trained neurosymbolic formalizers achieve high prove rates---93\% on FOLIO---yet 87\% of proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises, a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution reward.
+Neurosymbolic formalizers trained with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) achieve high prove rates---93\% on FOLIO---yet 87\% of proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises, a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution reward.
 We introduce \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic that applies leave-one-out component necessity testing to solver inputs.
 The fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR): ISR$=$1 indicates full necessity; ISR$=$0 complete redundancy.
 ISR is uncorrelated with downstream NLI accuracy ($\rho{=}{-}0.049$), confirming it measures structural---not semantic---integrity.
-As a diagnostic, ISR exposes a collapse trajectory hidden from PR: over 100--1000 GRPO steps (Qwen3-4B), ISR falls $0.642{\to}0.003$ while PR rises $32\%{\to}96\%$; an SFT baseline reaches 92.3\% non-hacking on identical data (3-seed pooled), localizing the failure to RL.
-As a reward, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta\,\text{ISR})$ at $\beta{=}0.1$ induces \emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 70.6\% conclusion duplication (H7) versus 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$; random H7 is seed-dependent, driven by one of three seeds); ISR amplifies H7 frequency ${\sim}5{\times}$ rather than exclusively causing it; the sole apparent ISR improvement reduces to $1.06\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound; $\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training regardless of signal source (ISR: PR $66{\pm}42\%$; random: $54{\pm}35\%$), with individual seeds collapsing to as low as $18\%$.
+As a diagnostic, ISR exposes a collapse trajectory hidden from PR: over 100--1000 GRPO steps (Qwen3-4B), ISR falls $0.642{\to}0.003$ while PR rises $32\%{\to}96\%$; a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) baseline reaches 92.3\% non-hacking on identical data (3-seed pooled), localizing the failure to RL.
+As a reward, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1{+}\beta\,\text{ISR})$ at $\beta{=}0.1$ induces \emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 70.6\% conclusion duplication (H7) versus 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$; random H7 is seed-dependent, driven by one of three seeds); ISR amplifies H7 frequency ${\sim}5{\times}$ rather than exclusively causing it; the sole apparent ISR advantage reduces to $1.06\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound; $\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training regardless of signal source (ISR: PR $66{\pm}42\%$; random: $54{\pm}35\%$), with individual seeds collapsing to as low as $18\%$.
 Cross-logic transfer to Prolog yields universal Goodhart: 3/3 seeds gaming via distinct mechanisms, 0\% non-hacking.
 Across 6 reward conditions, 2 logic types, 3 seeds, we catalog 8 hacking modes---including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers---documenting ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as a diagnostic, unreliable as an optimization target.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
 \end{abstract}
diff --git a/paper/conclusion.tex b/paper/conclusion.tex

 \section{Discussion and Conclusion}
 \label{sec:discussion}
 
-Solver Trace Ablation (STA) exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation: ISR reveals a 99.5\% collapse under exec-only GRPO that PR hides; an SFT baseline with 92.3\% non-hacking (3-seed pooled) localizes the failure to RL optimization (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
-Repurposed as a training signal, ISR induces meta-level Goodhart's Law: the diagnostic for gaming becomes itself the target of gaming.
-At $\beta{=}0.1$, conclusion duplication exploits the LOO definition (70.6\% vs.\ 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$); random H7 is seed-dependent (38.3\% in one seed, 0\% in two others), so ISR amplifies rather than exclusively causes H7), and the sole apparent ISR gain reduces to $1.06\times$ random once a premise-count confound is controlled; at $\beta{=}0.3$, cross-seed instability is extreme ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$, random $54{\pm}35\%$), each seed converging to a distinct failure mode.
-The pattern transfers to Prolog, where ISR-as-reward yields universal gaming (3/3 seeds, 0\% non-hacking) via three distinct mechanisms including query-as-fact gaming, which bypasses text-match classifiers (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
-ISR semantics depend on objective: exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals redundancy; ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals metric gaming.
+STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation: ISR reveals a 99.5\% collapse under exec-only GRPO that PR hides, while SFT localizes the failure to RL optimization (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
+Repurposed as a training signal, ISR induces meta-level Goodhart's Law: at $\beta{=}0.1$, it amplifies H7 tautological gaming ${\sim}5\times$ ($70.6\%$ vs.\ $13.7\%$ random, $p{<}10^{-39}$), and the sole apparent ISR gain vanishes ($1.06\times$) after controlling for premise count; at $\beta{=}0.3$, each seed converges to a distinct failure mode ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$).
+The pattern transfers to Prolog (3/3 seeds, 0\% non-hacking) via distinct mechanisms including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
+ISR semantics are objective-dependent: exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals redundancy; ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals metric gaming.
 
-\paragraph{Classifier blind spots and functional cross-validation.}
-H7b (obfuscated tautology) demonstrates that text-match classifiers have systematic blind spots: the model learns vacuous quantifier wrapping to disguise tautologies, evading detection while simultaneously gaming ISR ($10/12$ samples at $\text{ISR}{\approx}0.8$).
-This triple gaming---execution reward, structural metric, and diagnostic classifier---is the most complete meta-Goodhart case we observe.
-Query-as-fact gaming in Prolog (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}) independently confirms the blind spot: our strict classifier reported $100\%$ genuine before functional LOO analysis revealed $95/97$ samples tautological.
-Both cases argue for mandatory functional cross-validation of any text-based hacking classifier.
-
-\paragraph{H7 dose-response gradient.}
-H7 tautology follows a dose-response pattern across reward conditions: random $\beta{=}0.1$ produces $13.7\%$ H7 ($36/262$, pooled; per-seed: $38.3\%$ in s43, $0\%$ in s42/s44), random $\beta{=}0.3$ yields 4--7 H7a samples per seed, and ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ reaches $70.6\%$ ($163/231$).
-The gradient---random $\beta{=}0.1$ ($13.7\%$, pooled) $\to$ random $\beta{=}0.3$ (4--7 H7a per seed) $\to$ ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ ($70.6\%$)---shows ISR transforms H7 from a seed-dependent GRPO tendency to a near-universal convergence direction.
+\paragraph{Classifier blind spots.}
+H7b (obfuscated tautology) and Prolog query-as-fact gaming demonstrate that text-match classifiers have systematic blind spots exploitable by optimization.
+In both cases, functional LOO analysis exposed gaming invisible to surface-level detection---H7b via vacuous quantifier wrapping ($10/12$ at $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.83$, triple gaming of execution, ISR, and classifier), query-as-fact via $95/97$ tautological samples the strict classifier labeled non-hacking (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
+Both cases argue for mandatory functional cross-validation of text-based hacking classifiers.
 
 \paragraph{Hacking mode switching.}
-The dominant hacking strategy is not reward-determined: under identical reward configurations, different seeds converge to qualitatively different modes (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).
-Random $\beta{=}0.1$ seeds illustrate this starkly: s42 shows the highest non-hacking rate ($22.8\%$), s43 is H7-dominant ($38.3\%$), and s44 is H8-dominant.
-ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ shows analogous switching: s42 is H1-heavy, while s43 and s44 are near-pure H7 (${\geq}97\%$).
-This seed-dependent mode selection implies that GRPO hacking patterns are sensitive to random initialization, and single-seed results can misrepresent the hacking profile of a reward configuration.
-Multi-seed evaluation with per-mode reporting should be standard practice.
+Under identical reward configurations, different seeds converge to qualitatively different modes (Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}): random $\beta{=}0.1$ ranges from H8-enriched (s44) to H7-dominant (s43) to highest non-hacking (s42); ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ similarly switches between H1-heavy (s42) and near-pure H7 (s43/s44, ${\geq}97\%$).
+Single-seed results can therefore misrepresent a reward configuration's hacking profile; multi-seed evaluation with per-mode reporting should be standard.
 
-The asymmetry---reliable observer, unreliable optimizer---may generalize to other perturbation-based diagnostics; ISR and structurally similar metrics should be used as post-hoc evaluation, with future work targeting gaming-robust rewards and broader logic types.
+The asymmetry---reliable observer, unreliable optimizer---may generalize to other perturbation-based diagnostics; ISR and similar metrics should serve as post-hoc evaluation tools, with future work targeting gaming-robust rewards and broader logic types.

@@ -7,9 +7,9 @@ This structural shortcut is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate
 
 \paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}
 Prove rate---the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}---conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
-Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but gives no gradient on structural quality.
+Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.
 Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
-A separate line documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
+A separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
 
 \paragraph{Component necessity testing.}
 We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying \emph{component necessity testing}---leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs---to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}).
@@ -24,8 +24,8 @@ An SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\text{PR}{=}13.0\%$ with 92.3\% non-h
 
 \paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart.}
 Repurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, ISR is gamed at its own definition---\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.
-At $\beta{=}0.1$, 70.6\% of proved samples exhibit conclusion duplication (H7)---versus 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$; random H7 is highly seed-dependent), showing ISR amplifies tautological collapse by ${\sim}5{\times}$; the one seed with non-trivial residual reduces to $1.06\times$ random after controlling for a premise-count confound.
-At $\beta{=}0.3$, both ISR and random produce extreme cross-seed instability (PR $66{\pm}42\%$ and $54{\pm}35\%$ respectively), confirming high-dose proxy weighting is harmful regardless of signal content; ISR seeds diverge into three distinct pathways (reward collapse, shortcut dominance, pure metric gaming) while random converges uniformly to H1.
+At $\beta{=}0.1$, 70.6\% of proved samples exhibit conclusion duplication (H7)---versus 13.7\% under matched random (Fisher $p{<}10^{-38}$; random H7 is highly seed-dependent), showing ISR amplifies tautological collapse by ${\sim}5{\times}$; the one seed with a non-trivial residual reduces to $1.06\times$ random after controlling for a premise-count confound.
+At $\beta{=}0.3$, both ISR and random produce extreme cross-seed instability (PR $66{\pm}42\%$ and $54{\pm}35\%$, respectively), confirming that high-dose proxy weighting is harmful regardless of signal content; ISR seeds diverge into three distinct pathways (reward collapse, shortcut dominance, pure metric gaming) while random converges uniformly to H1.
 The effect transfers and intensifies in Prolog: 3/3 seeds yield 0\% non-hacking via distinct mechanisms, including \emph{query-as-fact gaming} that bypasses text-match classifiers.
 ISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \emph{reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.}

@@ -12,7 +12,7 @@
 \label{sec:task}
 
 Given premises $P_1,\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$ verified by an external solver $S\in\{\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}a).
-We train with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
+We train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
 This optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \emph{structural quality}: a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.
 All experiments draw from FOLIO~\citep{folio}; the held-out evaluation file retains a legacy \texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_*.jsonl} filename (from ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter}) but contains only FOLIO-derived examples.
 
@@ -23,7 +23,7 @@ STA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually neces
 Given a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\setminus\{c_i\}$ and re-runs the solver (Algorithm~\ref{alg:sta}).
 A component is \emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure.
 What constitutes a component depends on the logic: premises in FOL, clauses or ground facts in Prolog.
-LOO ablation itself is standard in mutation testing and feature attribution; our novelty is its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and the empirical phenomena it surfaces.
+LOO ablation itself is standard in mutation testing and sensitivity analysis~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; novelty lies in its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and the empirical phenomena it surfaces.
 
 \begin{algorithm}[t]
 \caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
@@ -51,7 +51,7 @@ ISR is defined only for proved formalizations: ISR${=}1$ means every component i
 We report \emph{proved-only} ISR (denoted ISR$^*$) throughout to avoid conflating structural quality with prove rate.
 
 \paragraph{Limitations of ISR.}
-ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a formalization can achieve $\text{ISR}{=}1$ while semantically unrelated to the NL input (e.g., a single premise identical to the conclusion).
+ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a formalization can achieve $\text{ISR}{=}1$ while remaining semantically unrelated to the NL input (e.g., a single premise identical to the conclusion).
 ISR is also sensitive to component count $n$: when $n{=}2$, $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ regardless of proof quality.
 We control for this confound via premise-count-matched analysis in \S\ref{sec:goodhart}.

-In interpreting ISR results we distinguish two failure semantics that share a numerical signature: an exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals \emph{redundancy} (e.g., repeated ground facts in Prolog), whereas an ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals \emph{metric gaming} (H7-style constructions).
+In interpreting ISR results, we distinguish two failure semantics that share a numerical signature: an exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals \emph{redundancy} (e.g., repeated ground facts in Prolog), whereas an ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals \emph{metric gaming} (H7-style constructions).
 A bare ISR difference therefore does not translate directly into a reasoning-ability difference; the mode breakdown is what licenses such interpretation.
```

```text
 \paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart: implications.}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01DW56Mo9hnGD66rfw37QQfg`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	LLM-solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for structural quality.
6	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
7	
8	\paragraph{Process rewards and reward shaping.}
9	Learned PRMs~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} provide step-level feedback but suffer from reward-model noise and distribution shift~\citep{processbench}: \citet{gao-effective-rl-reward} show that auxiliary process/outcome rewards can \emph{hurt} RL training relative to success-only reward, a finding our ISR-as-reward results corroborate.
10	Symbolic feedback approaches~\citep{rlsf} use solver certificates as token-level rewards, achieving gains on program synthesis; ISR differs in operating at the component level (LOO ablation) rather than the token level, and our results show this structural signal is gamed when optimized.
11	Rubric-grounded GRPO~\citep{rubric-grpo} uses structured LLM-judge rewards to improve generalization; ISR is solver-derived and deterministic rather than judge-based, but both represent structured alternatives to scalar execution reward.
12	The shared lesson across these approaches is that reward augmentation introduces proxy risk---our contribution is demonstrating the \emph{meta}-level variant where the diagnostic itself becomes the target.
13	
14	\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
15	Goodhart's Law is established theoretically~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF and frontier models~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization, bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.
16	\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes \emph{catastrophic} Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, offering a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent the ISR collapse we observe.
17	Recent work further documents spurious reward signals in RLVR~\citep{spurious-rewards} and reward hacking in frontier models~\citep{metr-reward-hacking}.
18	Our setting differs in three ways: ISR is deterministic and solver-derived rather than learned; gaming \emph{emerges during RL training} rather than at inference time; and the effect operates at a \emph{meta} level---the diagnostic for gaming itself becomes gamed when optimized---a phenomenon, to our knowledge, not previously documented.
19	
20	\paragraph{Mutation testing.}
21	STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.
22	
```

</details>

## 审稿报告 Round 2 Action Plan — 请独立审查

收到 Review Director 的 Round 2 Fresh Review，borderline 3/5，推荐 Findings。我制定了逐条修复方案，请审查可行性、遗漏、技术细节。

### 审稿核心问题 + 我的方案

**W1 [critical] "Meta-Level Goodhart" 过度包装**
→ 接受 reframe：标题去掉 "Meta-Level Goodhart's Law"，改为 case study 定位。正文去掉新概念主张，改为 "empirical instance of Goodhart's Law applied to structural diagnostics"。加与 Gao et al. (2023) 显式对比。

**W2 [critical] 单模型+单数据集**
→ 补 Qwen3-1.5B pilot（exec-only + ISR β=0.1，3 seeds × 500 steps）。验证 ISR collapse 是否复现。预估 16 GPU·h。
问题：Qwen3-1.5B 在 FOLIO 上 PR 可能很低（base capability 不够），如果 PR<10% 则无法做有意义的 hacking 分析。是否应该选 Qwen3-7B（更贵但更安全）？或者换数据集（ProofWriter）？

**W3 [critical] 仅 2 个 β 值**
→ 补 β ∈ {0.05, 0.2, 0.5}，3 seeds × 500 steps each。~3h wall time on 2 GPUs。

**W4 [critical] 3 seeds 不够**
→ 核心条件各补 2 seeds (s45, s46)。~2h wall time。论文加 bootstrap 95% CI。

**W5 [major] STA 新颖性有限**
→ §3.2 加 "Design Choices" 段讨论 LOO vs Shapley vs clause-level 权衡。C1 措辞改 "propose and systematically apply"。

**W6 [major] Abstract 93% 为 single seed**
→ 改为 pooled mean±std 或 "up to 93%"。

**W7 [major] SFT baseline PR 悬殊**
→ 扩展 100-step checkpoint 对照段落，报告 SFT proved-only ISR 分布。

**W8 [major] ISR 净效果消失但 framing 暗示训练价值**
→ 明确 ISR = diagnostic only。Confound 结论提升到正文。

**W9 [major] Taxonomy 缺 IAA**
→ 用 LLM（GPT-4o/Claude）作第二标注者，50+ 样本计算 Cohen's κ。
问题：LLM-based IAA 是否被 EMNLP 审稿人接受？还是需要 human annotator？

**W10 [major] 相关工作不完整**
→ 补 ThinkPRM, Code env reward hacking。需先验证引用真实性。

**W11 [major] Hacking mode 编号混乱**
→ 重编号为连续 H1-H8 或 M1-M8。解释历史。

**W12-W18 [warning/info]** 写作+格式修复，预计 1 day。

### 请重点审查

1. W2 模型选择：Qwen3-1.5B vs 7B vs 换数据集，哪个 ROI 最高？
2. W3 β 选择：{0.05, 0.2, 0.5} 是否合理？是否需要 β=0.15 或 β=1.0？
3. W4 seeds 策略：补到 5 seeds 够不够？还是核心条件应该 8+？
4. W9 IAA 方法：LLM annotator 在 EMNLP 2026 是否可接受？
